Let's design smooth objective for continuous h:
C_smooth(h) = (m + (1/β) log ∑ exp(β(c_j - m))) * dx, where c_j=corr full. Derivative: Let u_j = β(c_j - m); w_j = exp(u_j)/∑exp(β(c_j - m)) (with max included; can include j of max in sum). for _ in range(100):

     mask = h < 1.0

  if any mask:
     # distribute diff equally among mask but stop at bounds; simpler loop:
     # if one-sided: add diff/ count to all mask, clip, repeat

```

h=clip(h-mid,0,1)
```

Gradient loop optimization:

def weighted_gradient(x, beta, c=None, top_k=None):
    n=_G_N
    if c is None: c=corr_custom(x)
    m=c.max()
    shifted=beta*(c-m)
    shifted=np.clip(shifted, -745, 0)
    z=np.exp(shifted)
    if top_k is None: top_k=len(c)
    # select top k by c (or z)
    if top_k < len(c):
       idx=np.argpartition(c, -top_k)[-top_k:]
    else: idx=np.arange(len(c))
    weights_full = np.zeros(len(c)); weights_full[idx]=z[idx]/z[idx].sum()  # but if z underflow for top? top okay.
    g=np.zeros(n)
    for t in idx:
       w=weights_full[t]
       if w <= 1e-14: continue
       ell=int(t) - (n-1)
       if ell >=0:
          cnt=n-ell
          g[:cnt] += w * (1.0 - x[ell:])
          g[ell:] += -w * x[:cnt]
       else:
          a=-ell; cnt=n-a
          g[a:] += w * (1.0 - x[:cnt])
          g[a? no second? Wait earlier: second: g[:cnt] += -w * h[a:]? For ell<0, formula A_i first g[a:] += w*(1-h[:cnt]); second B_i? B_i = sum h_t alpha_{i-t}; for ell<0, second condition i+ell? Let's rederive loop for ℓ<0:
 c_ℓ = ∑_{i=-ℓ}^{n-1} h_i f_{i+ℓ}. Let a=-ℓ. Term for i=a..n-1: h_i f_{i-a}.
 Derivative:
 i as first: x_i * (1 - x_{i-a}); derivative wrt x_i: (1 - x_{i-a}) for i≥a => g[a:] += w*(1-x[:cnt]) where x[:cnt]=x[0:n-a].
 i as second: term h_j f_{j-a? wait if derivative wrt x_i appears as f_{j+ℓ}=f_i where j+ℓ=i => j=i-ℓ=i+a. Term h_{i+a}(1-x_i); derivative -h_{i+a} for valid i+a<n => i=0..n-a-1. So g[:cnt] += -w * x[a:]. Correct.
       return dx*g? For objective dx*softmax, yes multiply dx. In SLSQP, include dx.
```
For ell>=0: c_ℓ=∑_{i=0}^{n-1-ℓ} h_i f_{i+ℓ}. First derivative g[:cnt]+=w*(1-x[ℓ:]); second derivative g[ℓ:]+= -w*x[:cnt]. Correct.
Return g * dx.

Subgradient refinement function using same weighted gradient (smooth) and exact eval:
```
def gradient_refine(h, n, start_c, budget_remain, rng):
 c5=start_c; h=h.copy(); _G_N=n; _G_DX=2/n; _G_TGT=n/2
 for beta in [500,2000,5000] while time:
   for it in range(200):
     g=weighted_gradient(h, beta, top_k=32) # includes dx
     d = g - np.mean(g)
     # optional add random component if no improvement
     norm=np.linalg.norm(d)
     if norm < 1e-12: break
     u=d/norm
     improved=False
     steps=[0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2,0.4,0.8,0.005]
     for s in steps:
        trial=project_feasible(h - s*u, n)
        ct=eval_trial(trial)
        if ct < c5 - 1e-12:
           h=trial; c5=ct; improved=True; break
     if not improved:
       # try small random perturb plus gradient
       for sigma in [0.01,0.02,0.05]:
          trial=project_feasible(h + rng.normal(0,sigma,n) - 0.05? no)
          # maybe evaluate if improved
       # after some fails break
   # update best
 return h,c5
```

   i=one, j=zero.

   newmax=c.max()

Implement `project_feasible` with bisection:
```
def project_feasible(h,n):
    h=np.array(h,float).copy()
    target=n/2.0
    lo=-1.0; hi=1.0
    # Ensure h-mid sum increases as mid increases? clip(h-mid) decreasing in mid.
    for _ in range(80):
       mid=(lo+hi)/2
       s=np.sum(np.clip(h-mid,0,1))
       if s > target:
          lo=mid  # need larger mid to lower sum
       else:
          hi=mid
    h=np.clip(h-(lo+hi)/2,0,1)
    # residual adjust
    diff=target - np.sum(h)
    if abs(diff)>1e-14:
       if diff>0:
          mask = h < 1.0 - 1e-12
       else:
          mask = h > 1e-12
       if np.any(mask):
          idxs=np.where(mask)[0]
          # choose one with room
          # distribute equip: amount=diff/len; clip loop
          for _ in range(20):
              if abs(diff)<1e-15: break
              amask = h < 1.0 if diff>0 else h >0.0
              if not np.any(amask): break
              cnt=np.count_nonzero(amask)
              amp=diff/cnt
              # ensure not exceed
              # if amp too big, still add and clip then repeat
              h[amask]+=amp
              diff=target-np.sum(h)
       # final single adjust
       diff=target-np.sum(h)
       if abs(diff)>1e-15:
          if diff>0: idx=int(np.argmin(h))
          else: idx=int(np.argmax(h))
          h[idx]+=diff
          h=np.clip(h,0,1)
          # clip may change sum; then adjust a not-bound element
          diff=target-np.sum(h)
          if abs(diff)>1e-15:
             if diff>0:
                candidates=np.where(h<1.0)[0]
             else: candidates=np.where(h>0.0)[0]
             if candidates.size:
                idx=candidates[int(np.argmax(h?))]...
                h[idx]+=diff
                h=np.clip(h,0,1)
    return h
```
Need simpler robust water fill: Because bisection 80 should get sum within 1e-12. The final adjustment may break box if impossible? But target within [0,n] and h in [0,1], feasible. We can use a robust function to adjust residual by choosing elements with maximum room and clipping iteratively:
```
def enforce_sum(h,target):
 for _ in range(100):
   diff=target-np.sum(h)
   if abs(diff)<1e-14: break
   if diff>0: mask=h<1.0
   else: mask=h>0.0
   cnt=np.count_nonzero(mask)
   if cnt==0: break
   # If all mask room enough? amp=diff/cnt; if amp < min room for diff sign, do one pass. else iterate.
   h[mask]+=diff/cnt
   h=np.clip(h,0,1)
 return h
```
Bisection + this enough.

def refine_gradient(h,n,c5,rng,max_iter=100):
 _G_N=n; _G_DX=2/n; _G_TGT=n/2
 stalls=0
 for beta in [800,2000,5000,10000]:
   for it in range(max_iter):
     if remaining()<5: break
     g=weighted_gradient(h,beta,top_k=64)
     d=g - np.mean(g)
     # add small random dither to escape plateaus
     if it%20==5:
        d += rng.normal(0,0.05,n)
     norm=np.linalg.norm(d)
     if norm<1e-10: break
     u=d/norm
     improved=False
     steps=[1e-3,3e-3,1e-2,3e-2,1e-1,2e-1,4e-1,8e-1]
     for s in steps:
        trial=project_feasible(h - s*u,n)
        ct=eval_c5(trial)
        if ct < c5 - 1e-12:
           h=trial; c5=ct; improved=True; stalls=0; break
     if not improved:
        stalls+=1
        if stalls>10: break
        # try accept with tiny noisy step not improving? no; to escape, add noise and reset? We can make a noisy trial and if not worse than c5+1e-4 accept? Could wander. Maybe do:
        trial=project_feasible(h + rng.normal(0,0.02,n),n)
        ct=eval_c5(trial)
        if ct < c5 - 1e-12: update
        elif ct < c5 + 1e-6 and rng.rand()<0.1: h=trial; c5=ct? accepting worse bad. Avoid.
   # after each beta if no improvement break?
 return h,c5
```

```
def weighted_gradient(x, beta, top_k=None):
    n=_G_N
    if n<=0: return np.zeros(0)
    c=corr_custom(x)
    m=float(np.max(c))
    shifted=np.clip(beta*(c-m), -745, 0)
    e=np.exp(shifted)
    if top_k is None:
       idx=np.where(e>1e-12)[0]
       se=e[idx].sum()
       if se<=0: idx=np.argmax(c); eidx=...
    else:
       idx=np.argpartition(c, -top_k)[-top_k:] if top_k < len(c) else np.arange(len(c))
       se=e[idx].sum()
       if se<=0: idx=np.argmax(c) ...
    g=np.zeros(n)
    for ti in idx:
       w=e[ti]/se
       if w<=1e-12: continue
       ell=int(ti)-(n-1)
       ...
    return g
```

```

Informed move implementation:
```
def propose_pair(b,c,n,rng):
  # returns (i,j) or None
  topk = 4
  idx = np.argpartition(c, -topk)[-topk:]
  dec_one = []
  dec_zero = []
  for t in idx:
    ell=t-(n-1)
    if ell>=0:
      cnt=n-ell
      ones = b[:cnt]
      zeros = 1.0 - b[ell:]
      # i positions where ones and zeros
      mask_i = (b[:cnt] >0.5) & (b[ell:] <0.5)
      # for zero as second: j=ell..n-1 where b[j]=0 and b[j-ell]=1
      mask_j = (b[ell:] <0.5) & (b[:cnt] >0.5)
      # both essentially same positions? j=ell+i
    else:
      a=-ell; cnt=n-a
      # i positions a..n-1 where b[i]=1 and b[i-a]=0
      mask_i = (b[a:] >0.5) & (b[:cnt] <0.5)
      # j positions 0..cnt-1 where b[j]=0 and b[j+a]=1
      mask_j = (b[:cnt] <0.5) & (b[a:] >0.5)
    if any mask_i: append indices
  If dec_one and dec_zero: choose one from dec_one, one from dec_zero different
  else random.
```
Actually flipping i in dec_one 1->0 reduces first term. Flipping j in dec_zero 0->1 reduces term where j is second (since other one before). Both good.

But choosing pair of dec_one and dec_zero may further increase terms for the same lag if after flip j=1, if j also acts as first? It's okay.

     # if i==j continue
     c,h updated (b)

     if newmax < cur - 1e-9: accept=True

     elif newmax < cur + 1.0 and rng.rand()<0.01: accept=True
     if accept:

     moves+=1

Let's flesh code details:

Project:
```
def project_feasible(h, n, target=None):
    h = np.array(h, dtype=float).copy()
    if target is None: target = 0.5 * n
    lo=-1.0; hi=1.0
    # if h all within, bisection
    for _ in range(80):
       mid=(lo+hi)*0.5
       proj=np.clip(h-mid,0.0,1.0)
       s=float(np.sum(proj))
       if s > target:
          lo=mid
       else:
          hi=mid
    h=np.clip(h-(lo+hi)*0.5,0.0,1.0)
    # enforce closer
    for _ in range(50):
       diff=target - float(np.sum(h))
       if abs(diff) < 1e-14: break
       if diff > 0:
          mask = h < 1.0
       else:
          mask = h > 0.0
       cnt=int(np.count_nonzero(mask))
       if cnt == 0: break
       h[mask] += diff / cnt
       h = np.clip(h,0.0,1.0)
    diff=target - float(np.sum(h))
    if abs(diff)>1e-14:
       if diff>0:
          mask = h < 1.0
       else:
          mask = h > 0.0
       idxs=np.where(mask)[0]
       if idxs.size >0:
          # choose one with most room
          if diff>0:
             idx=int(np.argmin(h))  # max room to 1
          else:
             idx=int(np.argmax(h))
          h[idx]+=diff
          h=np.clip(h,0.0,1.0)
    return h
```
The bisection `lo` should be value that makes sum <= target? We set if s>target, need increase mid, so lo=mid. Else hi=mid. At end mid. Good.

Smooth obj/jac:
```
def smooth_obj(x):
    x=np.asarray(x,dtype=float)
    c=corr_np(x) # np.correlate
    m=float(np.max(c))
    shifted= _G_BETA*(c-m)
    shifted=np.clip(shifted, -745.0, 0.0)
    e=np.exp(shifted)
    s=float(np.sum(e))
    if s <=0: return m*_G_DX
    return (m + math.log(s)/_G_BETA)*_G_DX
```
`corr_np(x) = np.correlate(x,1-x,'full')`.

`weighted_gradient(x,beta,top_k=None)`:
```
n=int(_G_N)
c=corr_np(x)
m=max(c)
shifted=clip(beta*(c-m),-745,0)
e=exp(shifted)
if top_k is None:
   # include all with e>1e-14
   idx=np.flatnonzero(e > 1e-14)
   if idx.size==0: idx=np.array([int(np.argmax(c))])
   se=e[idx].sum()
   if se <=0: ...
else:
   k=int(top_k)
   if k >= len(c): idx=np.arange(len(c))
   else: idx=np.argpartition(c, -k)[-k:]
   se=e[idx].sum()
   if se <=0: idx=np.argmax(...); eidx...
g=zeros(n)
for t in idx:
 w=e[t]/se
 if w <=1e-14: continue
 ell=t-(n-1)
 if ell >=0:
   cnt=n-ell
   if cnt>0:
      g[:cnt] += w*(1.0 - x[ell:])
      g[ell:] += -w*x[:cnt]
 else:
   a=-ell; cnt=n-a
   if cnt>0:
      g[a:] += w*(1.0 - x[:cnt])
      g[:cnt] += -w*x[a:]
return g
```
Important for ell=0: first and second update overlap entire; correct (g += w*(1-x) + -w*x = w*(1-2x)). For ell negative, second `g[:cnt] += -w*x[a:]` correct. Need ensure `x[a:]` length cnt.

In SLSQP, we need jac full consistent. We can set `_G_JAC_TOP_K` global? Simpler have `smooth_jac` use `top_k=None` (full with underflow). For n≤512, okay. For n=640, maybe still. For n>512, set top_k=512 but objective full inconsistent. To avoid, for n>512 don't SLSQP.

`slsqp_once(h,n,beta,maxiter)`:
```
def slsqp_once(h,n,beta,maxiter):
    if maxiter <=0: return h
    global _G_N,_G_BETA,_G_TGT,_G_DX
    _G_N=n; _G_BETA=float(beta); _G_TGT=0.5*n; _G_DX=2.0/n
    bounds=[(0.0,1.0)]*n
    cons=[{'type':'eq','fun':con_sum}]
    try:
       res=minimize(smooth_obj, h, jac=smooth_jac, method='SLSQP', bounds=bounds, constraints=cons, options={'maxiter':int(maxiter),'ftol':1e-16,'iprint':0})
       x=res.x
       if not np.all(np.isfinite(x)): return h
       return project_feasible(x,n)
    except Exception:
       return h
```
`con_sum` uses `_G_TGT`.

`refine_gradient(h,n,c5,rng,max_iter)`:
```
def refine_gradient(h,n,c5,rng,max_iter):
    global _G_N,_G_BETA,_G_TGT,_G_DX
    _G_N=n; _G_TGT=0.5*n; _G_DX=2.0/n
    best_h=h.copy(); best_c=c5
    cur_h=h.copy(); cur_c=c5
    stalls=0
    for beta in [800.0, 2000.0, 4000.0, 8000.0]:
       top_k=64 if n<=320 else 96
       for it in range(max_iter):
          if remaining()<time_remaining_reserve: break
          g=weighted_gradient(cur_h,beta,top_k)
          d=g - np.mean(g)
          # add tiny random if stalls high
          if stalls > 5:
             d = d + rng.normal(0.0, 0.02, n)
          norm=float(np.linalg.norm(d))
          if norm < 1e-12: break
          u=d/norm
          improved=False
          steps=[0.002,0.005,0.01,0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2,0.4]
          if norm > 1.0: steps = [s * (norm/1.0? no)]
          # Since u normalized, okay.
          for s in steps:
             trial=project_feasible(cur_h - s*u, n)
             ct=eval_c5(trial)
             if ct < cur_c - 1e-12:
                cur_h=trial; cur_c=ct; improved=True; stalls=0
                if ct < best_c - 1e-12:
                   best_c=ct; best_h=trial.copy()
                break
          if not improved:
             stalls += 1
             if stalls > 25: break
             # occasionally non-improving noisy step to escape current (but best tracked)
             if it % 10 == 9:
                trial=project_feasible(cur_h + rng.normal(0.0, 0.05, n), n)
                ct=eval_c5(trial)
                # accept if not too bad (anneal)
                if ct < cur_c + 0.002 and rng.rand() < 0.5:
                   cur_h=trial; cur_c=ct
                elif ct < best_c - 1e-12:
                   cur_h=trial; cur_c=ct; best_c=ct; best_h=trial.copy()
          if cur_c < 0.38084: # target? Maybe continue? We'll not hardcode? Can
             pass
       if best_c <= target? not
       if stalls > 25: break
    return best_h, best_c
```
Accepting small worsening could make cur_c high; best tracked. But line search uses cur_c; maybe not issue.

If we stop once below target, might miss even lower but reward only need lower? "Lower C5 values are better" Maybe they compare to record, lower better. We should aim lower but okay. make_binary:
```
def make_binary_from_float(h,n):
 b = (h >=0.5).astype(float)
 target=int(n//2)
 s=int(np.sum(b))
 if s == target: return b
 if s < target:
    need=target-s
    zeros=np.where(b==0)[0]
    # choose zeros with largest h
    order=np.argsort(-h[zeros])[:need]
    b[zeros[order]]=1.0
 else:
    need=s-target
    ones=np.where(b==1)[0]
    order=np.argsort(h[ones])[:need]
    b[ones[order]]=0.0
 return b
```
This may not minimize C but fine.

return np.interp(xq,xp,src_h)
```

def optimize_resolution(h,n,c5,rng,allow_slsqp):
    _G_N=n; _G_TGT=0.5*n; _G_DX=2.0/n
    h=project_feasible(h,n)
    c5=eval_c5(h)
    best_h=h.copy(); best_c=c5
    if allow_slsqp and remaining()>45:
        betas=[40,100,250,600,1500,3500]
        if n<=256: base_max=120
        elif n<=384: base_max=70
        else: base_max=45
        no_improve=0
        for beta in betas:
            if remaining()<25: break
            maxit=base_max
            # scale down if time short
            if remaining()<60: maxit=min(maxit, 20)
            maxit=max(15,min(maxit, int(remaining()/3))) # no, /3 may huge
            hx=slsqp_once(h,n,beta,maxit)
            # if SLSQP result not improved, still try gradient from hx
            hx=project_feasible(hx,n)
            ch=eval_c5(hx)
            h,best_op = hx, ch
            if ch < best_c - 1e-12: best_c=ch; best_h=hx.copy(); h=hx; no_improve=0
            else: no_improve += 1
            if no_improve>=3 and beta>100:
                # still try one high? break maybe
                pass
    # refine
    if remaining()>20:
       h,c = hx if 'hx' else ...? We'll keep h current best.
       h_r,c_r=refine_gradient(best_h,n,best_c,rng, max_iter=150 if n<=320 else 80)
       if c_r<best_c: best_h=h_r; best_c=c_r
    return best_h,best_c
```

def binary_refine(h,n,rng,budget_cap):
    global _G_N,_G_TGT,_G_DX
    _G_N=n; _G_TGT=0.5*n; _G_DX=2.0/n
    b=project_feasible(make_binary_from_float(h,n),n)
    c=corr_np(b)
    cur=float(np.max(c)); curC=cur*_G_DX
    best_b=b.copy(); best_c=curC
    start_t=time.time()
    moves=0
    stall_count=0
    while remaining()>20 and (time.time()-start_t)<budget_cap:
       if moves % 100 ==0:
          ones=np.flatnonzero(b)
          zeros=np.flatnonzero(1-b)
          if len(ones)<n//2-2 or len(zeros)<n//2-2: reproject?
       i,j = propose_binary_pair(b,c,n,rng)
       if i is None: continue
       # store old values? bit flips
       old_i=b[i]; old_j=b[j]
       # apply
       c,b? apply_delta updates b internal. Let's make apply_delta return updated b? We can modify in place and return c.
       c=apply_delta_to_c(c,b,i,0.0) # if old_i=1
       c=apply_delta_to_c(c,b,j,1.0)
       newmax=float(np.max(c))
       accept=False
       if newmax < cur - 1e-9: accept=True
       elif abs(newmax-cur)<=1e-9 and rng.rand()<0.1: accept=True
       elif newmax < cur + 1.0 and rng.rand()<0.01: accept=True
       if accept:
          cur=newmax
          if cur*_G_DX < best_c - 1e-12: best_c=cur*_G_DX; best_b=b.copy(); stall_count=0
          else: stall_count += (1 if abs(newmax-cur?)? Actually if no best improvement but accepted, stallcount maybe unchanged)
       else:
          # revert: j back 0, i back 1
          c=apply_delta_to_c(c,b,j,0.0)
          c=apply_delta_to_c(c,b,i,1.0)
          stall_count +=1
       moves+=1
       if stall_count > 1000:
          # reset to best
          b=best_b.copy(); c=corr_np(b); cur=float(np.max(c)); stall_count=0
       if moves % 2000 ==0:
          c=corr_np(b) # correct drift
          cur=float(np.max(c))
    # ensure best sum
    best_b=project_feasible(best_b,n)
    return best_b,best_c
```
Note: apply_delta_to_c modifies b in place; when storing old_i not needed. But we need ensure old_i=1, old_j=0. Propose ensures. If b values not exactly 0/1 due project? binary values exactly. Good.

`propose_binary_pair`:
```
def propose_binary_pair(b,c,n,rng):
   topk=4
   if len(c)>topk:
      idx=np.argpartition(c, -topk)[-topk:]
   else: idx=np.arange(len(c))
   dec_one=[]; dec_zero=[]
   for t in idx:
      ell=int(t)-(n-1)
      if ell>=0:
         cnt=n-ell
         if cnt>0:
            m_on=(b[:cnt]>0.5)
            # zero as first at ell+
            mask_one = (b[:cnt]>0.5) & (b[ell: (ell+cnt)]<0.5) # b[ell:]
            mask_zero = (b[ell: (ell+cnt)]<0.5) & (b[:cnt]>0.5)
            # mask_one positions i; mask_zero positions j = ell+i; we need index j array
            if np.any(mask_one): dec_one.extend(np.where(mask_one)[0].tolist())
            if np.any(mask_zero):
               zz=np.where(mask_zero)[0]
               dec_zero.extend((zz+ell).tolist())
      else:
         a=-ell; cnt=n-a
         if cnt>0:
            # one as first: i=a+idx, paired with i-a zero
            mask_one = (b[a: (a+cnt)]>0.5) & (b[:cnt]<0.5)
            if any: dec_one.extend((idx+a).tolist())
            # zero as second: j=idx (0..cnt-1), paired j+a one
            mask_zero = (b[:cnt]<0.5) & (b[a: (a+cnt)]>0.5)
            if any: dec_zero.extend(np.where(mask_zero)[0].tolist())
   # limit lists
   if len(dec_one)>100: dec_one=rng.choice(dec_one,size=50,replace=False).tolist()
   if len(dec_zero)>100: dec_zero=rng.choice(dec_zero,size=50,replace=False).tolist()
   ones=np.flatnonzero(b>0.5); zeros=np.flatnonzero(b<0.5)
   if len(dec_one) and len(dec_zero):
      i=int(dec_one[rng.randint(len(dec_one))]); j=int(dec_zero[rng.randint(len(dec_zero))])
      if i==j: # fallback
   else:
      i=int(ones[rng.randint(len(ones))]); j=int(zeros[rng.randint(len(zeros))])
   if i==j: return None
   return i,j
```
`mask_one.extend(np.where(...).tolist())` works? np.where returns tuple of arrays; `.tolist()` on first array. Use `arr = np.nonzero(mask_one)[0]; dec_one.extend(arr.tolist())`.
Need avoid `b[ell: (ell+cnt)]` with cnt=n-ell gives b[ell:n], okay. For ell>=0, length of b[ell:] is cnt. For ell<0, b[a:a+cnt]=b[a:n].
If dec lists contain i that is actually zero? Should be one; for dec_one. Good.
If choose i one and j zero, they might be same if b at 0.5? no. If j in dec_zero is one? no.

`apply_delta_to_c` uses n from _G_N? We can pass n. Implementation:
```
def apply_delta_to_c(c,h,i,new):
    n=_G_N
    old=float(h[i])
    delta=float(new-old)
    if abs(delta)<1e-15: return c
    offs=np.arange(n)-i
    idx1=n-1+offs
    idx2=n-1-offs
    c[idx1] += delta * (1.0 - h)
    c[idx2] += -delta * h
    c[n-1] -= delta*delta
    h[i]=new
    return c
```
Potential bug: advanced indexing with `idx1` and values length n. For i at boundary, idx1/idx2 unique. Good.
But `c[idx1] += ...` when `idx1` is not contiguous; numpy handles. If idx1 contains duplicates? offs unique, no. Good.

    c5 = eval_c5(h)
    best_h = h.copy(); best_c = c5
    global _G_N,_G_TGT,_G_DX,_G_BETA

       elif n<=384: base=70
       else: base=45
       no_impr=0

          maxit=base

def smooth_h(h,sigma):
 n=len(h)
 if sigma <=0.1: return h.copy()
 r=int(2*ceil(3*sigma)+1)
 if r%2==0: r+=1
 x=np.arange(r)-(r-1)//2
 k=np.exp(-0.5*(x/sigma)**2)
 k /= np.sum(k)
 y=np.convolve(h,k,'same')
 return y / np.sum(y)* (n/2?) Wait smoothing preserves sum approximately? np.convolve same with normalized kernel preserves sum for full? For 'same' may lose edges. We'll project anyway. return y.
```

Detailed `refine_gradient` code:
```
def refine_gradient(h, n, c5, rng, max_iter):
    global _G_N, _G_TGT, _G_DX, _G_BETA
    _G_N = n; _G_TGT = 0.5*n; _G_DX = 2.0/n
    best_h = np.array(h, dtype=float).copy()
    best_c = float(c5)
    cur_h = best_h.copy()
    cur_c = best_c
    stalls = 0
    for beta in [600.0, 1500.0, 4000.0, 8000.0]:
        top_k = 48 if n <= 320 else 80
        for it in range(int(max_iter)):
            if remaining_time() < 5.0: break
            g = weighted_gradient(cur_h, beta, top_k)
            d = g - float(np.mean(g))
            if stalls > 6:
                d = d + rng.normal(0.0, 0.02, n)
            scale = float(np.max(np.abs(d)))
            if scale < 1e-12:
                break
            u = d / scale
            improved = False
            steps = [0.005,0.01,0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2,0.4,0.8]
            for s in steps:
                trial = project_feasible(cur_h - s*u, n)
                ct = eval_c5(trial)
                if ct < cur_c - 1e-12:
                    cur_h = trial; cur_c = ct; improved=True; stalls=0
                    if ct < best_c - 1e-12:
                        best_c = ct; best_h = trial.copy()
                    break
            if not improved:
                stalls += 1
                if stalls > 30: break
                if it % 8 == 7:
                    trial = project_feasible(cur_h + rng.normal(0.0, 0.04, n), n)
                    ct = eval_c5(trial)
                    if ct < best_c - 1e-12:
                        cur_h=trial; cur_c=ct; best_c=ct; best_h=trial.copy(); stalls=0
                    elif ct < best_c + 0.002 and rng.rand() < 0.25:
                        cur_h=trial; cur_c=ct
        if best_c <= 0.38084: # not hard stop
            pass
    return best_h, best_c
```
No hardcoded target necessary. Maybe if best_c very low, still okay.

Step sizes based on max norm; if u zero? handled.

`weighted_gradient` with top_k=48: It computes weights normalized over top_k selected by c. If c values near max, good.

Now, SLSQP starting from best may require setting `_G_BETA`; slsqp_once does. But `smooth_obj` uses `_G_DX`; set there.

Let's write `slsqp_once` with safeguards:
```
def slsqp_once(h,n,beta,maxiter):
    global _G_N,_G_BETA,_G_TGT,_G_DX
    _G_N=n ...
    x0 = np.asarray(h,dtype=float).copy()
    x0 = project_feasible(x0,n)
    # slight interior
    x0 = np.clip(x0, 1e-12, 1.0-1e-12)
    bounds = [(0.0,1.0)]*n
    cons = [{'type':'eq','fun':con_sum}]
    opt = {'maxiter':int(maxiter),'ftol':1e-16,'iprint':0}
    try:
       res = minimize(smooth_obj, x0, jac=smooth_jac, method='SLSQP', bounds=bounds, constraints=cons, options=opt)
       x = np.asarray(res.x,dtype=float)
       if not np.all(np.isfinite(x)): return h
       return project_feasible(x,n)
    except Exception:
       return h
```
Clipping to 1e-12 may make sum not exact; SLSQP constraint will handle, but initial slightly infeasible; okay. Could project after clip? We clip after project, sum changes by <=n*1e-12 ~1e-9, okay.

`smooth_obj` and `smooth_jac` assume `_G_N` is len(x); we set. If SLSQP passes x shape, okay.

Potential issue: SLSQP may call smooth_obj many times; `corr_np` direct O(n²). For n=512 and maxiter 45 maybe okay. But if beta high gradient support few, jac fast; obj still corr. SLSQP line search may evaluate objective at tenths; okay.

```
def make_golden_ratio(n):
 gr=(1+sqrt5)/2; h=np.zeros(n)
 for i in range(n):
   if (i*gr)%1.0 <0.5: h[i]=1.0
 return make_binary_from_float(h,n)
```

Code for propose:
```
def propose_binary_pair(b,c,n,rng):
    topk = 2 if c.size > 2 else c.size
    idxs = np.argpartition(c, -topk)[-topk:]
    for t in idxs:
        ell = int(t) - (n - 1)
        if ell >= 0:
            cnt = n - ell
            if cnt <=0: continue
            mask = (b[:cnt] > 0.5) & (b[ell:ell+cnt] < 0.5)
            arr = np.nonzero(mask)[0]
            if arr.size > 0 and rng.rand() < 0.75:
                r1 = int(arr[rng.randint(arr.size)])
                r2 = int(arr[rng.randint(arr.size)])
                j = ell + r2
                if 0 <= j < n and b[j] < 0.5:
                    return r1, j
        else:
            a = -ell
            cnt = n - a
            if cnt <=0: continue
            mask = (b[a:a+cnt] > 0.5) & (b[:cnt] < 0.5)
            arr = np.nonzero(mask)[0]
            if arr.size > 0 and rng.rand() < 0.75:
                r1 = int(arr[rng.randint(arr.size)])
                r2 = int(arr[rng.randint(arr.size)])
                i = a + r1
                j = r2
                if 0 <= i < n and 0 <= j < n and b[i] > 0.5 and b[j] < 0.5:
                    return i,j
    ones = np.nonzero(b > 0.5)[0]
    zeros = np.nonzero(b < 0.5)[0]
    if ones.size ==0 or zeros.size==0: return None
    i=int(ones[rng.randint(ones.size)]); j=int(zeros[rng.randint(zeros.size)])
    if i==j: return None
    return i,j
```
For ell>=0, j=ell+r2 is zero; i=r1 one; they may be same if ell=0? Top lags exclude ell=0 maybe c0 lower; if ell=0, mask empty because b and b same? (b>0.5)&(b<0.5) false. Good.

```
def binary_refine(h,n,rng,budget_cap):
    global _G_N,_G_TGT,_G_DX
    _G_N=n ...
    b=make_binary_from_float(h,n)
    c=corr_np(b)
    cur=float(np.max(c)); curC=cur*_G_DX
    best_b=b.copy(); best_c=curC; best_count=cur
    start_t=time.time()
    moves=0; stall=0
    while remaining_time()>20 and (time.time()-start_t)<budget_cap:
       if moves % 200 ==0:
          ones=... zeros=...
          # if lengths not target due drift, reproject?
       pair=propose...
       if pair is None: break
       i,j=pair
       # ensure one/zero
       if b[i]<0.5 or b[j]>0.5:
          # fallback
          ...
       c=apply_delta_to_c(c,b,i,0.0)
       c=apply_delta_to_c(c,b,j,1.0)
       newmax=float(np.max(c))
       newC=newmax*_G_DX
       accept=False
       if newmax < cur - 1e-9: accept=True
       elif abs(newmax-cur)<1e-9 and rng.rand()<0.15: accept=True
       elif newmax < cur + 1.0 and newmax < best_count + 4.0 and rng.rand()<0.02: accept=True
       if accept:
          cur=newmax
          if newC < best_c - 1e-12:
             best_c=newC; best_b=b.copy(); best_count=newmax; stall=0
       else:
          c=apply_delta_to_c(c,b,j,0.0)
          c=apply_delta_to_c(c,b,i,1.0)
          stall +=1
       moves+=1
       if stall > 800:
          b=best_b.copy(); c=corr_np(b); cur=float(np.max(c)); stall=0
       if moves % 1000 ==0:
          c=corr_np(b); cur=float(np.max(c))
    best_b=project_feasible(best_b,n)
    return best_b,best_c
```
`apply_delta_to_c` uses `_G_N` global; set. If we call with `i` after b changed? For revert, after first update b[i]=0,b[j]=1. Revert: apply_delta_to_c(c,b,j,0.0) (1->0), then i,1.0. Good. Need if float b values not exactly, new=0/1; delta okay.

In binary_refine, if projected best_b sum? binary exact. Good.

Potential issue in apply_delta: It uses `h` for other values; after sequential first update, h[i]=0; second update j uses h values including i=0. Correct. Revert order reverse? To restore old, apply inverse in reverse order? We did j 1->0 then i 0->1. Starting from both flipped, inverse returns. Since updates linear sequential, reverse order exact. Good.

Now, `apply_delta_to_c` diagonal correction `delta*delta` for bit flip ±1 =1. For revert deltas ±1. If old/new not exactly 0/1 due project, delta may not be ±1; but binary values should be.

    evals=[]

       if np.isfinite(cc): evals.append((cc, idx, hc))

One subtle bug: In `weighted_gradient`, for `top_k=None`, `idx = np.flatnonzero(e > 1e-14)`. If beta high, e for max entries 1, others maybe underflow. Good. If beta low, e maybe 1, all. If due to m, all shifted <=0, sum e >0. `se = e[idx].sum()`; okay. If `idx` sorted? `np.flatnonzero` sorted. Good.
For `top_k` specified, `idx = np.argpartition(c, -k)[-k:]` returns unsorted; okay. We compute `se = e[idx].sum()`. If some e underflow zero for top due to exactly not max? If top contains lower with e=0 and max not top? top selected by c includes max, so e=1. Good.

In gradient loop, for ell positive, `g[ell:] += -w * x[:cnt]`: if ell=0, first and second same full; okay. For ell negative, `g[a:] += w*(1-x[:cnt])`; `g[:cnt] += -w*x[a:]`; okay.

Now, `apply_delta_to_c`: For coordinate update, if `c` length and `h` length n. `idx1=n-1+offs` values 0..2n-2. `idx2=n-1-offs` also. But advanced indexing with `idx1` not sorted? okay. Update `c[n-1] -= delta*delta`. However the first/second updates both included diagonal j=i. For diagonal, first adds δ(1-old), second adds -δ old = δ -2δold? Wait applied diag δ(1-old) + (-δ old) = δ -2δold. Correction subtract δ². Actual diag δ(1-old-new). Check old=0.5,new=0.7 δ=0.2: applied=0.2*(0.5)-0.2*0.5=0; actual=0.2*(1-1.2)=-0.04. Difference -0.04? Our subtract δ²=0.04 => -0.04 correct. Wait applied - actual = 0 - (-0.04)=0.04, so subtract 0.04. formula works. Good.

```python
import numpy as np
import time
import math
from scipy.optimize import minimize

_G_N = 0
_G_BETA = 1.0
_G_DX = 1.0
_G_TGT = 0.0
_G_START = 0.0
_G_BUDGET = 1300.0


def remaining_time():
    return max(0.0, _G_BUDGET - (time.time() - _G_START) - 3.0)


def project_feasible(h, n, target=None):
    h = np.array(h, dtype=float).copy()
    if target is None:
        target = 0.5 * n

    lo = -1.0
    hi = 1.0
    for _ in range(80):
        mid = 0.5 * (lo + hi)
        s = float(np.sum(np.clip(h - mid, 0.0, 1.0)))
        if s > target:
            lo = mid
        else:
            hi = mid

    h = np.clip(h - 0.5 * (lo + hi), 0.0, 1.0)

    for _ in range(50):
        diff = target - float(np.sum(h))
        if abs(diff) < 1e-14:
            break
        if diff > 0.0:
            mask = h < 1.0
        else:
            mask = h > 0.0
        cnt = int(np.count_nonzero(mask))
        if cnt == 0:
            break
        h[mask] += diff / cnt
        h = np.clip(h, 0.0, 1.0)

    diff = target - float(np.sum(h))
    if abs(diff) > 1e-14:
        if diff > 0.0:
            idx = int(np.argmin(h))
        else:
            idx = int(np.argmax(h))
        h[idx] += diff
        h = np.clip(h, 0.0, 1.0)

    return h


def interp_to(src_h, src_n, dst_n):
    src_h = np.asarray(src_h, dtype=float)
    if src_n == dst_n:
        return src_h.copy()
    xp = (np.arange(src_n) + 0.5) * (2.0 / src_n)
    xq = (np.arange(dst_n) + 0.5) * (2.0 / dst_n)
    return np.interp(xq, xp, src_h)


def eval_c5(h):
    n = int(len(h))
    if n <= 0:
        return float("inf")
    dx = 2.0 / n
    c = np.correlate(h, 1.0 - h, mode="full")
    return float(np.max(c)) * dx


def corr_np(h):
    return np.correlate(np.asarray(h, dtype=float), 1.0 - np.asarray(h, dtype=float), mode="full")


def weighted_gradient(x, beta, top_k=None):
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=float)
    n = int(_G_N)
    if n <= 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=float)

    c = corr_np(x)
    m = float(np.max(c))
    shifted = np.clip(beta * (c - m), -745.0, 0.0)
    e = np.exp(shifted)

    if top_k is None:
        idx = np.flatnonzero(e > 1e-14)
        if idx.size == 0:
            idx = np.array([int(np.argmax(c))], dtype=int)
        se = float(np.sum(e[idx]))
        if se <= 0.0:
            idx = np.array([int(np.argmax(c))], dtype=int)
            se = float(np.sum(e[idx]))
    else:
        k = int(top_k)
        if k <= 0:
            k = 1
        if k >= c.size:
            idx = np.arange(c.size, dtype=int)
        else:
            idx = np.argpartition(c, -k)[-k:]
        se = float(np.sum(e[idx]))
        if se <= 0.0:
            idx = np.array([int(np.argmax(c))], dtype=int)
            se = float(np.sum(e[idx]))

    g = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
    for t in idx:
        w = float(e[t]) / se
        if w <= 1e-14:
            continue
        ell = int(t) - (n - 1)

        if ell >= 0:
            cnt = n - ell
            if cnt > 0:
                g[:cnt] += w * (1.0 - x[ell:])
                g[ell:] += -w * x[:cnt]
        else:
            a = -ell
            cnt = n - a
            if cnt > 0:
                g[a:] += w * (1.0 - x[:cnt])
                g[:cnt] += -w * x[a:]

    return g


def smooth_obj(x):
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=float)
    c = corr_np(x)
    m = float(np.max(c))
    shifted = np.clip(_G_BETA * (c - m), -745.0, 0.0)
    e = np.exp(shifted)
    s = float(np.sum(e))
    if s <= 0.0:
        return m * _G_DX
    return (m + math.log(s) / _G_BETA) * _G_DX


def smooth_jac(x):
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=float)
    g = weighted_gradient(x, _G_BETA, None)
    return g * _G_DX


def con_sum(x):
    return float(np.sum(x)) - _G_TGT


def slsqp_once(h, n, beta, maxiter):
    global _G_N, _G_BETA, _G_TGT, _G_DX

    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=float).copy()
    n = int(n)
    if n <= 0:
        return h

    _G_N = n
    _G_BETA = float(beta)
    _G_TGT = 0.5 * n
    _G_DX = 2.0 / n

    x0 = project_feasible(h, n)
    x0 = np.clip(x0, 1e-12, 1.0 - 1e-12)

    bounds = [(0.0, 1.0)] * n
    constraints = [{"type": "eq", "fun": con_sum}]

    try:
        res = minimize(
            smooth_obj,
            x0,
            jac=smooth_jac,
            method="SLSQP",
            bounds=bounds,
            constraints=constraints,
            options={"maxiter": int(max(1, int(maxiter))), "ftol": 1e-16, "iprint": 0},
        )
        x = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=float)
        if not np.all(np.isfinite(x)):
            return h
        return project_feasible(x, n)
    except Exception:
        return h


def refine_gradient(h, n, c5, rng, max_iter):
    global _G_N, _G_TGT, _G_DX, _G_BETA

    n = int(n)
    _G_N = n
    _G_TGT = 0.5 * n
    _G_DX = 2.0 / n

    best_h = np.array(h, dtype=float).copy()
    best_c = float(c5)
    cur_h = best_h.copy()
    cur_c = best_c
    stalls = 0

    for beta in (600.0, 1500.0, 4000.0, 8000.0):
        top_k = 48 if n <= 320 else 80
        for it in range(int(max_iter)):
            if remaining_time() < 5.0:
                break

            g = weighted_gradient(cur_h, beta, top_k)
            d = g - float(np.mean(g))
            if stalls > 6:
                d = d + rng.normal(0.0, 0.02, n)

            scale = float(np.max(np.abs(d)))
            if scale < 1e-12:
                break
            u = d / scale

            improved = False
            steps = [0.005, 0.01, 0.02, 0.05, 0.1, 0.2, 0.4, 0.8]
            for s in steps:
                trial = project_feasible(cur_h - s * u, n)
                ct = eval_c5(trial)
                if ct < cur_c - 1e-12:
                    cur_h = trial
                    cur_c = ct
                    improved = True
                    stalls = 0
                    if ct < best_c - 1e-12:
                        best_c = ct
                        best_h = trial.copy()
                    break

            if not improved:
                stalls += 1
                if stalls > 30:
                    break
                if it % 8 == 7:
                    trial = project_feasible(cur_h + rng.normal(0.0, 0.04, n), n)
                    ct = eval_c5(trial)
                    if ct < best_c - 1e-12:
                        cur_h = trial
                        cur_c = ct
                        best_c = ct
                        best_h = trial.copy()
                        stalls = 0
                    elif ct < best_c + 0.002 and rng.rand() < 0.25:
                        cur_h = trial
                        cur_c = ct

    return best_h, best_c


def optimize_resolution(h, n, rng):
    global _G_N, _G_TGT, _G_DX, _G_BETA

    n = int(n)
    _G_N = n
    _G_TGT = 0.5 * n
    _G_DX = 2.0 / n

    h = project_feasible(h, n)
    c5 = eval_c5(h)
    best_h = h.copy()
    best_c = c5

    if n <= 512 and remaining_time() > 50.0:
        betas = [60.0, 150.0, 400.0, 1000.0, 2500.0, 6000.0]
        if n <= 256:
            base_max = 120
        elif n <= 384:
            base_max = 70
        else:
            base_max = 45

        for beta in betas:
            if remaining_time() < 25.0:
                break
            rem = remaining_time()
            maxit = base_max
            if rem < 80.0:
                maxit = min(maxit, 22)
            if rem < 45.0:
                maxit = min(maxit, 12)
            maxit = max(10, int(min(base_max, rem / 4.0)))

            hx = slsqp_once(h, n, beta, maxit)
            hx = project_feasible(hx, n)
            ch = eval_c5(hx)
            h = hx
            if ch < best_c - 1e-12:
                best_c = ch
                best_h = hx.copy()

    if remaining_time() > 25.0:
        max_iter = 180 if n <= 320 else (100 if n <= 512 else 40)
        h2, c2 = refine_gradient(best_h, n, best_c, rng, max_iter)
        if c2 < best_c - 1e-12:
            best_h = h2
            best_c = c2

    if n <= 512 and remaining_time() > 25.0:
        hx = slsqp_once(best_h, n, 8000.0, 20)
        hx = project_feasible(hx, n)
        ch = eval_c5(hx)
        if ch < best_c - 1e-12:
            best_h = hx
            best_c = ch

    return best_h, best_c


def make_binary_from_float(h, n):
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=float)
    b = (h >= 0.5).astype(float)
    target = int(n // 2)
    s = int(np.sum(b))

    if s == target:
        return b

    if s < target:
        need = target - s
        zeros = np.nonzero(b == 0.0)[0]
        if zeros.size >= need:
            order = np.argsort(-h[zeros])[:need]
            b[zeros[order]] = 1.0
    else:
        need = s - target
        ones = np.nonzero(b == 1.0)[0]
        if ones.size >= need:
            order = np.argsort(h[ones])[:need]
            b[ones[order]] = 0.0

    return b


def make_random_bin(rng, n):
    h = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
    idx = rng.permutation(n)
    h[idx[:n // 2]] = 1.0
    return h


def make_thue_morse(n):
    h = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
    for i in range(n):
        v = i
        cnt = 0
        while v:
            cnt ^= (v & 1)
            v >>= 1
        if cnt == 1:
            h[i] = 1.0
    return make_binary_from_float(h, n)


def make_golden_ratio(n):
    gr = (1.0 + math.sqrt(5.0)) / 2.0
    h = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
    for i in range(n):
        if ((i * gr) % 1.0) < 0.5:
            h[i] = 1.0
    return make_binary_from_float(h, n)


def symmetrize(h):
    return 0.5 * (h + h[::-1])


def smooth_h(h, sigma):
    n = int(len(h))
    if sigma <= 0.1:
        return np.array(h, dtype=float).copy()
    r = int(2 * math.ceil(3.0 * sigma) + 1)
    if r % 2 == 0:
        r += 1
    x = np.arange(r) - (r - 1) // 2
    k = np.exp(-0.5 * (x / sigma) ** 2)
    k /= float(np.sum(k))
    return np.convolve(np.asarray(h, dtype=float), k, mode="same")


def apply_delta_to_c(c, h, i, new):
    n = int(_G_N)
    old = float(h[i])
    delta = float(new) - old
    if abs(delta) < 1e-15:
        return c

    offs = np.arange(n) - i
    idx1 = n - 1 + offs
    idx2 = n - 1 - offs

    c[idx1] += delta * (1.0 - h)
    c[idx2] += -delta * h
    c[n - 1] -= delta * delta
    h[i] = float(new)
    return c


def propose_binary_pair(b, c, n, rng):
    topk = 2 if c.size > 2 else c.size
    idxs = np.argpartition(c, -topk)[-topk:]

    for t in idxs:
        ell = int(t) - (n - 1)
        if ell >= 0:
            cnt = n - ell
            if cnt <= 0:
                continue
            mask = (b[:cnt] > 0.5) & (b[ell:ell + cnt] < 0.5)
            arr = np.nonzero(mask)[0]
            if arr.size > 0 and rng.rand() < 0.75:
                r1 = int(arr[rng.randint(arr.size)])
                r2 = int(arr[rng.randint(arr.size)])
                j = ell + r2
                if 0 <= j < n and b[j] < 0.5:
                    return r1, j
        else:
            a = -ell
            cnt = n - a
            if cnt <= 0:
                continue
            mask = (b[a:a + cnt] > 0.5) & (b[:cnt] < 0.5)
            arr = np.nonzero(mask)[0]
            if arr.size > 0 and rng.rand() < 0.75:
                r1 = int(arr[rng.randint(arr.size)])
                r2 = int(arr[rng.randint(arr.size)])
                i = a + r1
                j = r2
                if 0 <= i < n and 0 <= j < n and b[i] > 0.5 and b[j] < 0.5:
                    return i, j

    ones = np.nonzero(b > 0.5)[0]
    zeros = np.nonzero(b < 0.5)[0]
    if ones.size == 0 or zeros.size == 0:
        return None

    i = int(ones[rng.randint(ones.size)])
    j = int(zeros[rng.randint(zeros.size)])
    if i == j:
        return None
    return i, j


def binary_refine(h, n, rng, budget_cap):
    global _G_N, _G_TGT, _G_DX

    n = int(n)
    _G_N = n
    _G_TGT = 0.5 * n
    _G_DX = 2.0 / n

    b = make_binary_from_float(np.asarray(h, dtype=float), n)
    c = corr_np(b)
    cur = float(np.max(c))
    cur_c = cur * _G_DX

    best_b = b.copy()
    best_c = cur_c
    best_count = cur

    start_t = time.time()
    moves = 0
    stall = 0

    while remaining_time() > 20.0 and (time.time() - start_t) < float(budget_cap):
        pair = propose_binary_pair(b, c, n, rng)
        if pair is None:
            break
        i, j = pair

        if b[i] < 0.5 or b[j] > 0.5:
            ones = np.nonzero(b > 0.5)[0]
            zeros = np.nonzero(b < 0.5)[0]
            if ones.size == 0 or zeros.size == 0:
                break
            i = int(ones[rng.randint(ones.size)])
            j = int(zeros[rng.randint(zeros.size)])
            if i == j:
                continue

        c = apply_delta_to_c(c, b, i, 0.0)
        c = apply_delta_to_c(c, b, j, 1.0)

        newmax = float(np.max(c))
        new_c = newmax * _G_DX

        accept = False
        if newmax < cur - 1e-9:
            accept = True
        elif abs(newmax - cur) <= 1e-9 and rng.rand() < 0.15:
            accept = True
        elif newmax < cur + 1.0 and newmax < best_count + 4.0 and rng.rand() < 0.02:
            accept = True

        if accept:
            cur = newmax
            if new_c < best_c - 1e-12:
                best_c = new_c
                best_count = newmax
                best_b = b.copy()
                stall = 0
        else:
            c = apply_delta_to_c(c, b, j, 0.0)
            c = apply_delta_to_c(c, b, i, 1.0)
            stall += 1

        moves += 1
        if stall > 800:
            b = best_b.copy()
            c = corr_np(b)
            cur = float(np.max(c))
            stall = 0

        if moves % 1000 == 0:
            c = corr_np(b)
            cur = float(np.max(c))

    best_b = project_feasible(best_b, n)
    return best_b, best_c


def run(seed=42, budget_s=1300, **kwargs):
    global _G_N, _G_BETA, _G_TGT, _G_DX, _G_START, _G_BUDGET

    _G_START = time.time()
    _G_BUDGET = float(budget_s)
    _G_BETA = 1.0

    rng = np.random.RandomState(seed)

    best_h = None
    best_n = 0
    best_c = float("inf")

    try:
        parent_h = np.array(initial_h_values, dtype=float).copy()
        parent_n = int(len(parent_h))
        if parent_n > 0:
            parent_h = project_feasible(parent_h, parent_n)
            pn = parent_n
            dh = 2.0 / pn
            pc = eval_c5(parent_h)
            best_h = parent_h.copy()
            best_n = parent_n
            best_c = pc
    except Exception:
        best_n = 256
        best_h = np.full(best_n, 0.5, dtype=float)
        best_h = project_feasible(best_h, best_n)
        best_c = eval_c5(best_h)

    try:
        ev = float(evaluate_erdos_solution(best_h))
        if np.isfinite(ev) and ev < best_c:
            best_c = ev
    except Exception:
        pass

    parent_n = best_n
    n_process = []
    seen = []
    for nn in [parent_n, 256, 320, 384, 448, 512, 640]:
        if nn % 2 == 0 and 128 <= nn <= 640 and nn not in seen:
            n_process.append(nn)
            seen.append(nn)

    for n in n_process:
        if remaining_time() < 45.0:
            break

        starts = []
        h_interp = project_feasible(interp_to(best_h, best_n, n), n)
        starts.append(h_interp)

        if n <= 384:
            starts.append(project_feasible(smooth_h(h_interp, 1.5), n))
            starts.append(project_feasible(symmetrize(h_interp), n))
            starts.append(make_binary_from_float(h_interp, n))

        if best_c > 0.45:
            starts.append(project_feasible(make_random_bin(rng, n), n))
            starts.append(project_feasible(make_thue_morse(n), n))
            starts.append(project_feasible(make_golden_ratio(n), n))

        if remaining_time() > 500.0 and n <= 384:
            starts.append(project_feasible(h_interp + rng.normal(0.0, 0.04, n), n))
            starts.append(project_feasible(h_interp + rng.normal(0.0, 0.08, n), n))

        evals = []
        for idx, hs in enumerate(starts):
            if hs is None or len(hs) != n:
                continue
            hs = project_feasible(hs, n)
            cc = eval_c5(hs)
            if np.isfinite(cc):
                evals.append((cc, idx, hs))

        evals.sort()

        max_starts = 4 if (n <= 256 and remaining_time() > 600.0) else (3 if n <= 384 else 2)
        for item in evals[:max_starts]:
            cc = item[0]
            hs = item[2]
            if cc > best_c + 0.08 and cc > 0.39:
                continue
            if remaining_time() < 45.0:
                break
            hnew, cnew = optimize_resolution(hs, n, rng)
            if cnew < best_c - 1e-12:
                best_c = cnew
                best_h = hnew.copy()
                best_n = int(len(hnew))

    if remaining_time() > 25.0:
        hnew, cnew = refine_gradient(best_h, best_n, best_c, rng, 80)
        if cnew < best_c - 1e-12:
            best_c = cnew
            best_h = hnew.copy()
            best_n = int(len(hnew))

    if remaining_time() > 80.0:
        n_bin = best_n if 256 <= best_n <= 512 else 384
        hb, cb = binary_refine(best_h, n_bin, rng, min(60.0, remaining_time() - 30.0))
        if cb < best_c - 1e-12:
            best_c = cb
            best_h = hb.copy()
            best_n = int(len(hb))

        if remaining_time() > 90.0 and n_bin != 512:
            h512 = project_feasible(interp_to(best_h, best_n, 512), 512)
            hb, cb = binary_refine(h512, 512, rng, min(60.0, remaining_time() - 30.0))
            if cb < best_c - 1e-12:
                best_c = cb
                best_h = hb.copy()
                best_n = 512

    if remaining_time() > 15.0:
        hnew, cnew = refine_gradient(best_h, best_n, best_c, rng, 30)
        if cnew < best_c - 1e-12:
            best_c = cnew
            best_h = hnew.copy()
            best_n = int(len(hnew))

    best_h = project_feasible(best_h, best_n)
    final_c = eval_c5(best_h)

    try:
        ev = float(evaluate_erdos_solution(best_h))
        if np.isfinite(ev) and ev < final_c:
            final_c = ev
    except Exception:
        pass

    return best_h, float(final_c), int(best_n)
```
